People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I, well... I guess I'm here because I've been having a little bit of trouble at work lately. It's probably not a huge deal, but... I'm an artist, and recently, my artist statements and my notes for clients—and even just what I say when we have meetings—have been getting a little, I don't know, jumbled?

Well, um... I guess I have been feeling pretty good lately? Like, for stretches, I just feel so... lit up, I guess? Almost unstoppable, really. It’s not that I’m trying to be difficult, it’s just that I care so much about the work, you know?

Um... well... I want to say no, because I know it sounds so much better if it only happens when I'm tired and working too hard. I really want to give you the right answer here. 

But... if I'm being completely honest—and I know I should be honest with you—there was this one time. Just one time, really! It was a quieter month, and I was actually sleeping pretty normally. I wasn't feeling all... unstoppable and energized like before. I was just trying to have a regular routine, you know? Being good. 

But... I still kept hearing those comments. Like, they sounded like they were coming from behind the supply shelves in the studio. That went on for a few weeks, even though I was resting. 

I'm so sorry, I know that probably sounds really disappointing. I really thought if I just slept more, it would all go away. Does that... does that make things a lot worse? I really hope you don't think less of me for it. I'm trying so hard to keep everything normal.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, goodness, no. Um... I really hope I didn't make it sound earlier like I completely lose control! I would feel just awful if I acted that way around other people.

I'm trying really hard to be clear, I promise, I just don't want to seem unprofessional to my clients. \n\nIs that... does that sound like an okay reason to come in?\n\nDoctor: When other people have trouble following you, is it because your words come out slurred or hard to pronounce?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, um... no, I don't think so. My speech isn't slurred or anything like that. I actually try really hard to enunciate, especially when I'm talking to clients, because I really want to come across as professional. \n\nIt's more just... the topics, I think? Like, the actual words I'm saying are fine, but the way I jump from one idea to the next is what seems to throw them off. At least, I really hope I'm not slurring! I would feel so embarrassed if I was. Does that make sense? I promise I'm usually very articulate.\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual and still felt energetic?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I have been sleeping a little bit less lately, but it's really not a bad thing! I've just been so inspired and productive. \n\nSometimes I'll be working late in the studio, and I'll maybe just get two or three hours of sleep. But the funny thing is, I wake up feeling completely bright and ready to keep going. I'm not tired at all, which is weird because usually I need a full eight hours to function. But right now, I just feel really energized and focused on my art. \n\nIs it okay that I'm working that much? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job for my clients, you know?\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you been feeling unusually happy, euphoric, or irritable compared with your usual self?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I have been feeling pretty good lately? Like, for stretches, I just feel so... lit up, I guess? Almost unstoppable, really. It’s like every idea I have for a piece is just brilliant, and I’m so excited to get it out into the world. It’s actually a really nice feeling, being that inspired. \n\nBut, um, I guess... well, sometimes if a collaborator or a client makes even just a small comment, or asks for a tiny change, I can get a little... snappy? I try really hard not to, I promise, but sometimes I just react in a way that feels a lot bigger than the situation actually calls for. It’s not that I’m trying to be difficult, it’s just that I care so much about the work, you know? \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound terrible to work with. I always apologize if I get too defensive. Is it normal to feel that strongly about your work?\n\nDoctor: Have you started any new medications, recreational drugs, or supplements recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, um... definitely not. I haven't started anything new at all. I don't do any recreational drugs or anything like that. I really try to take good care of my health, you know? I want to make sure I'm always clear-headed and reliable for my clients. \n\nI know artists sometimes get this stereotype of, um, partying or being a little reckless, but I promise I'm very responsible. I haven't even taken any new vitamins or supplements or anything. Just trying to stay focused and do good work naturally. \n\nIs that... is that what you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period like this before?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yeah, I guess I have. It's not a new thing, exactly. These stretches where I'm just so energized and, you know, maybe a little irritable with the lack of sleep and juggling all these projects... it's been happening on and off for about ten months now, I think. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make it sound like I'm out of control or anything! I mean, I've still been getting my work done and keeping my clients happy, mostly. I just... I try to channel all that extra energy into my art, so it's actually been kind of productive. But it has been going on for a little while. Is... is that okay? I mean, is it bad that it's been happening for ten months? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by telling you.\n\nDoctor: During these periods, have you ever needed to be hospitalized or had such severe trouble that you couldn’t manage your work or daily life?\n\nPatient: Hospitalized? Oh, gosh, no. No, I promise, nothing like that. I really try to keep everything together, you know? I want to be someone people can rely on. \n\nBut, um... I guess... well, if I'm being completely honest, maybe it has gotten a little bit tricky sometimes? Just slightly. Like, I have missed a couple of paid deadlines. Not on purpose! I just got so caught up in the ideas, and time kind of got away from me. And... well, sometimes in meetings with collaborators, they've seemed a little confused by what I'm saying. I guess my thoughts just move a bit faster than I can explain them. \n\nAnd, um, there was this one exhibition that got postponed. They said my notes and proposals just... weren't making enough sense to use. I was really trying my best, I promise. I just had so many concepts flowing at once. \n\nI feel so terrible about it, honestly. I really want to be professional and easy to work with. Does... does that make me sound completely incompetent? I'm really trying to get better at managing it all, I swear.\n\nDoctor: During these periods, have you ever had hallucinations or believed things that other people said were not true?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess... I mean, I don't want you to think I'm crazy or anything, because I'm really not. I'm just very creative, you know? But, um... when I get really, really charged up about my work, and I'm hardly sleeping because I'm just doing so much... sometimes things get a little... intense. \n\nLike, I'd see these coffee stains on my desk, or notice certain phrases in emails from clients, and... well, I felt really sure they were actually instructions for me. Like hidden messages guiding my art. And, um, sometimes when I was alone in the studio, I'd hear this woman's voice just... commenting on what I was doing. \n\nI know that probably sounds really strange, and I'm so sorry if it does. I really try to just ignore it and focus on my work. It only happens when I'm super busy and not sleeping much, I promise. Is... is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm not giving you the wrong impression of me.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices or hidden messages ever happened when you were not in one of these energized, low-sleep periods?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I want to say no, because I know it sounds so much better if it only happens when I'm tired and working too hard. I really want to give you the right answer here. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest—and I know I should be honest with you—there was this one time. Just one time, really! It was a quieter month, and I was actually sleeping pretty normally. I wasn't feeling all... unstoppable and energized like before. I was just trying to have a regular routine, you know? Being good. \n\nBut... I still kept hearing those comments. Like, they sounded like they were coming from behind the supply shelves in the studio. And I was still seeing things... like, reading these hidden warnings into just ordinary studio materials, like the way the paint tubes were arranged. That went on for a few weeks, even though I was resting. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that probably sounds really disappointing. I really thought if I just slept more, it would all go away. Does that... does that make things a lot worse? I really hope you don't think less of me for it. I'm trying so hard to keep everything normal.\n\nDoctor: Have there ever been any hallucinations or fixed false beliefs when you were not having any mood symptoms like being unusually energized, euphoric, or irritable?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, actually, that's... that's exactly what I was just trying to explain. I hope I didn't confuse you earlier! I really want to be clear. \n\nLike I mentioned, there was that one month where things were... well, quiet. I wasn't feeling all euphoric or energized, and I definitely wasn't irritable. I was just... normal. I was sleeping fine, trying to just be a good, reliable person, you know? \n\nBut... yeah, during those few weeks, even without any of that mood stuff, I was still hearing those comments. Like they were coming from right behind the supply shelves. And I was still seeing those hidden warnings in the paint tubes and things. \n\nI really wish I could say it only happened when I was overtired, because I know that sounds so much more... manageable. I'm so sorry if I'm repeating myself, I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right. Is that what you meant? I really hope this doesn't mean I'm doing something wrong.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed or lost interest in things?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, actually. I can definitely say no to that one! \n\nI mean, I really love my art so much. Even when I'm just having a normal, quiet month, I still want to be in the studio and create. I don't think I've ever just... lost interest in things for weeks at a time, or gotten really, really down like you're describing. I always try to stay positive and keep busy with my projects. \n\nSo, no, I haven't had anything like that. I hope... I hope that's a good sign? I mean, it's a good thing that I don't get depressed, right? I'm really trying my best to keep a good attitude about everything.\n\nDoctor: Have your hallucinations or fixed false beliefs ever continued for at least two weeks when your mood was completely back to normal?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes. I think... wait, is this the same thing we were just talking about? I am so, so sorry if I'm being confusing or if I didn't explain it right earlier! I really want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. \n\nBut yes, just to be completely sure I'm answering you properly... during that quieter month I mentioned, my mood was completely back to normal. I was sleeping normally, and I wasn't feeling unstoppable or anything. But for several weeks during that time, I still kept hearing those comments from behind the shelves, and I was still reading those hidden warnings into ordinary studio materials. \n\nI hope it's okay that I'm repeating that. Did I answer it right this time? I really want to be helpful and make sure I'm doing this properly!\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least one week when your mood was so elevated or irritable that you were noticeably more talkative, more active, or unusually confident than usual?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose there have been times like that. I hope it doesn't make me sound like a difficult person to work with, because I really do try to be a good collaborator! \n\nBut, um, sometimes... I guess for stretches of a week or so, I'll feel just... lit up, you know? Like I'm almost unstoppable and every single idea I have for a piece is just brilliant. I get so much done, and it feels really good, honestly. \n\nThe only thing is... well, sometimes during those really productive stretches, if someone I'm working with makes even a small comment or suggestion, I might... snap at them. Just a little bit! It just feels bigger than the situation warrants, and I always feel terrible about it afterward. I really don't mean to be irritable, it just sort of bubbles up when I'm feeling that energized. \n\nIs that... is that the kind of thing you mean? I hope that doesn't sound too awful. I really do try to keep things harmonious in the studio!\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least one week when you felt so energized or irritable that you were getting much less done because your behavior became clearly out of control, like spending much more money, taking major risks, or acting in a way others found obviously abnormal?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. Um... I really hope I didn't make it sound earlier like I completely lose control! I would feel just awful if I acted that way around other people. \n\nWhen I get that... that burst of energy I was talking about, I'm actually getting a lot *more* done, not less. I would never want to be a burden or act in a way that people thought was... well, abnormal. \n\nSo, no, nothing out of control like that. I really pride myself on keeping things together and being a professional. That's... that's exactly what you want to hear, right? Um... yes, I think we touched on this a little bit earlier, actually. I'm sorry if I'm repeating myself, I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need!
